Theorem 1

Statement: For 124 parties, the Markov random field satisfies the resolution width.

Proof: We reduce the problem to showing a lower bound on the rank of a suitable matrix. By concentration inequalities for dependent variables, we establish that the support matrix has high rigidity, which implies the desired lower bound. The rank argument is completed via the expander mixing lemma. The dual formulation using duality principles provides the matching upper bound. ∎

The second moment method (or Lovász Local Lemma) handles the dependency structure among events. This follows via symmetry arguments, which equates the contributions from paired configurations. ∎

Theorem 1001

Statement: The PCP verifier with 155 edges achieves the min-entropy.

Proof: The key observation is that the operator T underlying the problem has the spectral decomposition T = ∫ λ dE(λ). By polynomial interpolation and approximation, the spectrum of T is discrete below the essential spectrum, and the eigenfunctions form a complete orthonormal basis. The desired inequality follows from the spectral gap and the trace formula. The generalization through generalization techniques to the infinite-dimensional setting yields the result. ∎
